and

$$f_{a}(\theta) = \frac{-g(\theta - \theta_{0}) - g(-\theta - \theta_{0})}{2} = \frac{-\sum_{\substack{j=0 \ j \text{ odd}}}^{n} \beta_{j} \mathbf{T}_{j} (\sin(\theta - \theta_{0})) - \sum_{\substack{j=0 \ j \text{ odd}}}^{n} \beta_{j} \mathbf{T}_{j} (\sin(-\theta - \theta_{0}))}{2}$$

$$-\sum_{\substack{j=0 \ j \text{ odd}}}^{n} \beta_{j} \sin(j\theta - j\theta_{0}) (-1)^{\frac{j-1}{2}} - \sum_{\substack{j=0 \ j \text{ odd}}}^{n} \beta_{j} \sin(-j\theta - j\theta_{0}) (-1)^{\frac{j-1}{2}}$$

$$= \frac{-\sum_{\substack{j=0 \ j \text{ odd}}}^{n} \beta_{j} \cos(j\theta) \sin(j\theta_{0}) (-1)^{\frac{j-1}{2}}}{2}.$$

$$= \sum_{\substack{j=0 \ j \text{ odd}}}^{n} \beta_{j} \cos(j\theta) \sin(j\theta_{0}) (-1)^{\frac{j-1}{2}}.$$
(314)

The above functions actually depend on various parameters such as  $\theta_0$ ,  $\varphi$  and  $\epsilon$ , but when their values are clear from the context, we will suppress these dependences for presentational purposes. The qualitative behaviors of these functions are shown in Table 5 and Figure 3.

#### D.2 Branch marking

Recall from Section 4.1 that our preparation of the discretized inverse state uses the quantum walk operator. When the input matrix has the spectral decomposition

$$A = \sum_{u} \lambda_u |\phi_u\rangle\langle\phi_u|, \tag{315}$$

the walk operator has the corresponding spectral decomposition

$$W = \sum_{u} \left( e^{i\theta_{u,+}} |\phi_{u,Y+}\rangle \langle \phi_{u,Y+}| + e^{i\theta_{u,-}} |\phi_{u,Y-}\rangle \langle \phi_{u,Y-}| \right), \qquad \theta_{u,\pm} = \pm \arccos\left(\frac{\lambda_u}{\alpha_A}\right).$$
(316)

Here, each eigenvector  $|\phi_u\rangle$  of the coefficient matrix splits into 2 eigenvectors  $|\phi_{u,Y\pm}\rangle$  of the quantum walk operator, and our primary goal is to ensure that these two branches undergo the exact same computation in GPE. This way, they can be merged back to recover  $|\phi_{u,0}\rangle$ , on which we perform the eigenvalue inversion  $\frac{1}{\lambda_u}$ .

## PDF page 73


Figure 3: Illustration of the qualitative behavior of functions used in branch marking (left panel) and GPE (right panel).

To this end, we choose the block encoding normalization factor  $\alpha_A \geq 2 \|A\|$ , so that

$$\frac{\lambda_u}{\alpha_A} \in \left[ -\frac{1}{2}, \frac{1}{2} \right]. \tag{317}$$

Then in the quantum walk operator, this eigenvalue corresponds to two eigenphases

$$\operatorname{arccos}\left(\frac{\lambda_u}{\alpha_A}\right) \in \left[\frac{\pi}{3}, \frac{2\pi}{3}\right], \quad -\operatorname{arccos}\left(\frac{\lambda_u}{\alpha_A}\right) \in \left[-\frac{2\pi}{3}, -\frac{\pi}{3}\right].$$
(318)

To distinguish between these cases, we can implement the periodic threshold functions with  $\theta_0 = \varphi = \frac{\pi}{6}$ . This gives

$$f_c\left(\pm \arccos\left(\frac{\lambda_u}{\alpha_A}\right)\right) \approx \pm 1, \qquad f_a\left(\pm \arccos\left(\frac{\lambda_u}{\alpha_A}\right)\right) \approx 0.$$
 (319)

We now carefully analyze the error. Specifically, we consider the two periodic threshold functions  $f_c(\theta)$  and  $f_a(\theta)$  with  $\theta_0 = \varphi = \frac{\pi}{6}$  whose actions are given by Table 5 and Figure 3. By symmetry, it suffices to focus on the interval  $\left[\frac{\pi}{3}, \frac{2\pi}{3}\right]$ . Recall that  $f_c(\theta) = \frac{g(\theta - \theta_0) - g(-\theta - \theta_0)}{2}$  and  $f_a(\theta) = \frac{-g(\theta - \theta_0) - g(-\theta - \theta_0)}{2}$ , which implies

$$f_c^2(\theta) + f_a^2(\theta) = \frac{g^2(\theta - \theta_0) + g^2(-\theta - \theta_0)}{2}.$$
 (320)

For all  $-\pi \leq \theta \leq \pi$ , we have from Table 5 that  $|g(\theta - \theta_0)|$ ,  $|g(-\theta - \theta_0)| \leq 1$ , so  $f_c^2(\theta) + f_a^2(\theta) \leq 1$  always holds. Also we know from Table 5 that  $1 - \epsilon \leq f_a(0) \leq 1$ . Furthermore, for the target interval where  $\frac{\pi}{3} \leq \theta \leq \frac{2\pi}{3}$ , we have  $1 - \epsilon \leq g(\theta - \theta_0) \leq 1$  and  $-1 \leq g(-\theta - \theta_0) \leq -1 + \epsilon$ , which implies  $1 - \epsilon \leq f_c(\theta) \leq 1$ . To summarize,

$$\begin{cases}
f_a^2(\theta) + f_c^2(\theta) \le 1, & \forall \theta \in [-\pi, \pi], \\
1 - \epsilon \le f_a(0) \le 1, & \\
1 - \epsilon \le f_c(\theta) \le 1, & \forall \theta \in \left[\frac{\pi}{3}, \frac{2\pi}{3}\right], \\
-1 \le f_c(\theta) \le -1 + \epsilon, & \forall \theta \in \left[-\frac{2\pi}{3}, -\frac{\pi}{3}\right].
\end{cases}$$
(321)

## PDF page 74


We now explain how to satisfy the requirements of [Lemma 20.](#page-69-2) We first run the sum-of-squares method [\[41\]](#page-86-15) to obtain functions *fb,*1(*θ*), and *fd,*1(*θ*) from

$$f_{a,1}(\theta) = f_a(\theta), \qquad f_{c,1}(\theta) = f_c(\theta), \tag{322}$$

where

$$\begin{cases} f_{a,1}^2(\theta) + f_{b,1}^2(\theta) + f_{c,1}^2(\theta) + f_{d,1}^2(\theta) = 1, & \forall \theta \in [-\pi, \pi], \\ f_{a,1}^2(0) + f_{b,1}^2(0) = 1. \end{cases}$$
(323)

Then, we let

$$f_{a,2}(\theta) = f_{a,1}(\theta)f_{a,1}(0) + f_{b,1}(\theta)f_{b,1}(0), \qquad f_{c,2}(\theta) = f_{c,1}(\theta).$$
(324)

Note that by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,

$$f_{a,2}^{2}(\theta) + f_{c,2}^{2}(\theta) = (f_{a,1}(\theta)f_{a,1}(0) + f_{b,1}(\theta)f_{b,1}(0))^{2} + f_{c,1}^{2}(\theta)$$

$$\leq \left(f_{a,1}^{2}(\theta) + f_{b,1}^{2}(\theta)\right) \left(f_{a,1}^{2}(0) + f_{b,1}^{2}(0)\right) + f_{c,1}^{2}(\theta)$$

$$= f_{a,1}^{2}(\theta) + f_{b,1}^{2}(\theta) + f_{c,1}^{2}(\theta) \leq 1.$$
(325)

Thus, we can rerun the sum-of-squares method to get functions *fb,*2(*θ*), and *fd,*2(*θ*). These functions now satisfy

$$\begin{cases}
f_{a,2}^2(\theta) + f_{b,2}^2(\theta) + f_{c,2}^2(\theta) + f_{d,2}^2(\theta) = 1, & \forall \theta \in [-\pi, \pi], \\
f_{a,2}(0) = 1,
\end{cases}$$
(326)

as desired, resulting in the QSP operator

$$\sum_{v} (f_{a,2}(\theta_v)I + if_{b,2}(\theta_v)Z + if_{c,2}(\theta_v)X + if_{d,2}(\theta_v)Y) \otimes |\phi_v\rangle\langle\phi_v|, \tag{327}$$

where *v* goes through all 2-tuple (*u,* ±).

It is clear that *fc,*<sup>2</sup> = *fc,*<sup>1</sup> = *fc*, so we introduce no additional error to the function *fc*:

$$\begin{cases}
1 - \epsilon \le f_{c,2}(\theta) \le 1, & \forall \theta \in \left[\frac{\pi}{3}, \frac{2\pi}{3}\right], \\
-1 \le f_{c,2}(\theta) \le -1 + \epsilon, & \forall \theta \in \left[-\frac{2\pi}{3}, -\frac{\pi}{3}\right].
\end{cases}$$
(328)

As for the remaining components,

$$f_{a,2}^2(\theta) + f_{b,2}^2(\theta) + f_{d,2}^2(\theta) = 1 - f_{c,2}^2(\theta) \le 1 - (1 - \epsilon)^2 = 2\epsilon - \epsilon^2, \qquad \forall \theta \in \left[ -\frac{2\pi}{3}, -\frac{\pi}{3} \right] \cup \left[ \frac{\pi}{3}, \frac{2\pi}{3} \right]. \tag{329}$$

To proceed, we need the following distance formula for matrices expanded in the Pauli basis.

**Lemma 21** (Matrix distance in the Pauli basis)**.** *For real vectors β* = [*β<sup>a</sup> β<sup>b</sup> β<sup>c</sup> βd*] *and γ* = [*γ<sup>a</sup> γ<sup>b</sup> γ<sup>c</sup> γd*]*,*

$$\|(\beta_a I + i\beta_b Z + i\beta_c X + i\beta_d Y) - (\gamma_a I + i\gamma_b Z + i\gamma_c X + i\gamma_d Y)\| = \|\beta - \gamma\|. \tag{330}$$

*Proof.* We know that the Hermitian matrix

$$(\beta_b - \gamma_b)Z + (\beta_c - \gamma_c)X + (\beta_d - \gamma_d)Y \tag{331}$$

has eigenvalues

$$\pm \sqrt{(\beta_b - \gamma_b)^2 + (\beta_c - \gamma_c)^2 + (\beta_d - \gamma_d)^2}.$$
 (332)

The claim then follows by rescaling by *i* and shifting by *β<sup>a</sup>* − *γa*.

## PDF page 75


When *θ* ∈ h *π* 3 *,* 2*π* 3 i , our ideal operator is *iX*, so the error is bounded by

$$||iX - ((f_{a,2}(\theta)I + if_{b,2}(\theta)Z + if_{c,2}(\theta)X + if_{d,2}(\theta)Y))||$$

$$\leq \sqrt{(1 - f_{c,2}(\theta))^2 + f_{a,2}^2(\theta) + f_{b,2}^2(\theta) + f_{d,2}^2(\theta)}$$

$$\leq \sqrt{\epsilon^2 + 2\epsilon - \epsilon^2} = \sqrt{2\epsilon}.$$
(333)

Similarly, when *θ* ∈ h − 2*π* 3 *,* − *π* 3 i , our ideal operator is −*iX*, so the error is again bounded by

$$\|-iX - ((f_{a,2}(\theta)I + if_{b,2}(\theta)Z + if_{c,2}(\theta)X + if_{d,2}(\theta)Y))\|$$

$$\leq \sqrt{(-1 - f_{c,2}(\theta))^2 + f_{a,2}^2(\theta) + f_{b,2}^2(\theta) + f_{d,2}^2(\theta)}$$

$$\leq \sqrt{\epsilon^2 + 2\epsilon - \epsilon^2} = \sqrt{2\epsilon}.$$
(334)

This establishes the following:

**Proposition 22** (Branch marking)**.** *Let A/α<sup>A</sup> be block encoded by O<sup>A</sup> with normalization factor α<sup>A</sup>* ≥ 2 ∥*A*∥*. Let A*|*ϕu*⟩ = *λu*|*ϕu*⟩ *and W*|*ϕu,Y* <sup>±</sup>⟩ = *e* <sup>±</sup>*<sup>i</sup>* arccos *λu αA* |*ϕu,Y* <sup>±</sup>⟩ *be the corresponding eigenpairs of A and the quantum walk operator W. For any ϵ >* 0*, the isometry*

$$|+\rangle|+\rangle|\phi_{u,Y\pm}\rangle \mapsto |\xi_{u,\pm}\rangle|\phi_{u,Y\pm}\rangle, \qquad ||\xi_{u,\pm}\rangle - |+\rangle|\pm\rangle|| \le \epsilon$$
 (335)

*can be implemented using*

$$\mathbf{O}\left(\log\left(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\right)\right) \tag{336}$$

*queries to OA.*

*Proof.* By [Lemma 20](#page-69-2) and the proceeding analysis, we can implement the operator

$$V = \sum_{u} \left( F(\theta_{u,+}) \otimes |\phi_{u,Y+}\rangle \langle \phi_{u,Y+}| + F(\theta_{u,-}) \otimes |\phi_{u,Y-}\rangle \langle \phi_{u,Y-}| \right), \tag{337}$$

such that <sup>∥</sup>*F*(*θu,*±) <sup>−</sup> (±*iX*)∥ ≤ <sup>√</sup> 2*ϵ* for all *u*. Then, the controlled unitary

$$|0\rangle\langle 0| \otimes I + |1\rangle\langle 1| \otimes (-iV) \tag{338}$$

implements the mapping

$$|+\rangle|+\rangle|\phi_{u,Y\pm}\rangle \mapsto |\pm\rangle|+\rangle|\phi_{u,Y\pm}\rangle$$
 (339)

to accuracy <sup>√</sup> <sup>2</sup>*ϵ*. The query complexity follows now from Eq. [\(311\)](#page-70-0) and the rescaling <sup>√</sup> 2*ϵ* 7→ *ϵ*.

## D.3 Branch marked gapped phase estimation

In GPE, our goal is to distinguish between eigenvalues of the input matrix within intervals

$$\frac{\lambda_u}{\alpha_A} \in \left[ -\frac{\gamma}{\rho}, \frac{\gamma}{\rho} \right] \quad \text{and} \quad \frac{\lambda_u}{\alpha_A} \in (-1, -\gamma] \cup [\gamma, 1).$$
(340)

When we construct the quantum walk operator, each eigenvalue is split into two eigenphases. For the positive branch, our goal is to distinguish between

$$\operatorname{arccos}\left(\frac{\lambda_u}{\alpha_A}\right) \in \left[\operatorname{arccos}\left(\frac{\gamma}{\rho}\right), \pi - \operatorname{arccos}\left(\frac{\gamma}{\rho}\right)\right]$$
(341)

## PDF page 76


and

$$\arccos\left(\frac{\lambda_u}{\alpha_A}\right) \in (0,\arccos(\gamma)] \cup [\pi - \arccos(\gamma),\pi),$$
 (342)

whereas for the negative branch, we need to differentiate

$$-\arccos\left(\frac{\lambda_u}{\alpha_A}\right) \in \left[-\pi + \arccos\left(\frac{\gamma}{\rho}\right), -\arccos\left(\frac{\gamma}{\rho}\right)\right]$$
 (343)

and

$$-\arccos\left(\frac{\lambda_u}{\alpha_A}\right) \in (-\pi, -\pi + \arccos(\gamma)] \cup [-\arccos(\gamma), 0). \tag{344}$$

Additionally, we need to ensure that GPE has exactly the same behavior on eigenphases with opposite signs.

Let us consider the positive branch first (corresponding to arccos *λu α<sup>A</sup>* ). To this end, we choose

$$\theta_0 = \frac{\arccos\left(\gamma\right) + \arccos\left(\frac{\gamma}{\rho}\right)}{2}, \qquad \varphi = \frac{\arccos\left(\frac{\gamma}{\rho}\right) - \arccos\left(\gamma\right)}{2}. \tag{345}$$

Then, we implement

$$\sum_{u} \left( f_a(\theta_{u,+}) I + i f_b(\theta_{u,+}) Z + i f_c(\theta_{u,+}) X + i f_d(\theta_{u,+}) Y \right) \otimes |\phi_{u,Y+}\rangle \langle \phi_{u,Y+}|, \tag{346}$$

where

$$f_c(\theta) \approx \begin{cases} 0, & \theta \in (0, \arccos(\gamma)], \\ 1, & \theta \in \left[\arccos\left(\frac{\gamma}{\rho}\right), \pi - \arccos\left(\frac{\gamma}{\rho}\right)\right], \\ 0, & \theta \in \left[\pi - \arccos(\gamma), \pi\right), \end{cases}$$
(347)

and

$$f_{a}(\theta) \approx \begin{cases} 1, & \theta \in (0, \arccos(\gamma)], \\ 0, & \theta \in \left[\arccos\left(\frac{\gamma}{\rho}\right), \pi - \arccos\left(\frac{\gamma}{\rho}\right)\right], \\ -1, & \theta \in \left[\pi - \arccos(\gamma), \pi\right). \end{cases}$$
(348)

Note that *fa*(*θu,*+) ≈ ±1 has different signs for eigenvalue of the input matrix *λ<sup>u</sup>* ≷ 0 with opposite signs, and the function values are also quite different in the transition band. But this is acceptable since this auxiliary information will be uncomputed in the end.

However, this choice of functions will have a different behavior on the negative branch. To address this issue, we instead aim to perform

$$\sum_{v} (f_a(-\theta_{u,-})I + if_b(-\theta_{u,-})Z + if_c(-\theta_{u,-})X + if_d(-\theta_{u,-})Y) \otimes |\phi_{u,Y-}\rangle \langle \phi_{u,Y-}|.$$
 (349)

That is, the action of this new operator on negative eigenphases is exactly the same as that of the above operator on positive eigenphases. Moreover, the new polynomials automatically satisfy the requirements. In particular, it holds that *fc*(−*θ*) = −*fc*(*θ*), *fa*(−*θ*) = *fa*(*θ*).

Now we discuss error analysis. Without loss of generality, we focus on the distinguishment of [0*,* arccos (*γ*)] and <sup>h</sup> arccos *γ ρ , π* 2 i , since the other cases have exactly the same error by symmetry. Proceeding as in the previous subsection, we consider *fc*(*θ*) = *<sup>g</sup>*(*θ*−*θ*0)−*g*(−*θ*−*θ*0) 2 and *fa*(*θ*) = −*g*(*θ*−*θ*0)−*g*(−*θ*−*θ*0) <sup>2</sup> where

$$f_c^2(\theta) + f_a^2(\theta) = \frac{g^2(\theta - \theta_0) + g^2(-\theta - \theta_0)}{2}.$$
 (350)

## PDF page 77


We also know from [Table 5](#page-71-1) that *f* 2 *c* (*θ*) + *f* 2 *a* (*θ*) ≤ 1 and 1 − *ϵ* ≤ *fa*(0) ≤ 1. Furthermore, we have − *ϵ* <sup>2</sup> ≤ *fc*(*θ*) ≤ *ϵ* 2 and 1 − *ϵ* ≤ *fa*(*θ*) ≤ 1 for the target interval 0 ≤ *θ* ≤ arccos (*γ*), and 1 − *ϵ* ≤ *fc*(*θ*) ≤ 1 and − *ϵ* <sup>2</sup> ≤ *fa*(*θ*) ≤ *ϵ* 2 for the target interval arccos *γ ρ* ≤ *θ* ≤ *π* 2 . To summarize,

$$\begin{cases}
f_a^2(\theta) + f_c^2(\theta) \leq 1, & \forall \theta \in [-\pi, \pi], \\
1 - \epsilon \leq f_a(0) \leq 1, \\
|f_c(\theta)| \leq \frac{\epsilon}{2}, 1 - \epsilon \leq f_a(\theta) \leq 1, & \forall \theta \in [0, \arccos(\gamma)], \\
1 - \epsilon \leq f_c(\theta) \leq 1, & |f_a(\theta)| \leq \frac{\epsilon}{2}, & \forall \theta \in \left[\arccos\left(\frac{\gamma}{\rho}\right), \frac{\pi}{2}\right].
\end{cases}$$
(351)

When *θ* ∈ h arccos *γ ρ , π* 2 i , our ideal operator is *iX*, so the error can be bounded in a similar way as in the previous subsection

$$||iX - ((f_{a,2}(\theta)I + if_{b,2}(\theta)Z + if_{c,2}(\theta)X + if_{d,2}(\theta)Y))||$$

$$\leq \sqrt{(1 - f_{c,2}(\theta))^2 + f_{a,2}^2(\theta) + f_{b,2}^2(\theta) + f_{d,2}^2(\theta)}$$

$$\leq \sqrt{\epsilon^2 + 2\epsilon - \epsilon^2} = \sqrt{2\epsilon}.$$
(352)

Let us now consider *θ* ∈ [0*,* arccos (*γ*)]. We let

$$f_{a,1}(\theta) = f_a(\theta), \qquad f_{c,1}(\theta) = f_c(\theta), \tag{353}$$

and run the sum-of-squares method [\[41\]](#page-86-15) to obtain

$$\begin{cases}
f_{a,1}^2(\theta) + f_{b,1}^2(\theta) + f_{c,1}^2(\theta) + f_{d,1}^2(\theta) = 1, & \forall \theta \in [-\pi, \pi], \\
f_{a,1}^2(0) + f_{b,1}^2(0) = 1.
\end{cases}$$
(354)

Continuing, we define

$$f_{a,2}(\theta) = f_{a,1}(\theta)f_{a,1}(0) + f_{b,1}(\theta)f_{b,1}(0), \qquad f_{c,2}(\theta) = f_{c,1}(\theta).$$
 (355)

and rerun the sum-of-squares method to get

$$\begin{cases}
f_{a,2}^2(\theta) + f_{b,2}^2(\theta) + f_{c,2}^2(\theta) + f_{d,2}^2(\theta) = 1, & \forall \theta \in [-\pi, \pi], \\
f_{a,2}(0) = 1.
\end{cases}$$
(356)

This results in

$$\sum_{u} (f_{a,2}(\theta_{u,+})I + if_{b,2}(\theta_{u,+})Z + if_{c,2}(\theta_{u,+})X + if_{d,2}(\theta_{u,+})Y) \otimes |\phi_{u,Y+}\rangle \langle \phi_{u,Y+}|.$$
 (357)

Now we have

$$|f_{a,2}(\theta) - f_{a,1}(\theta)| = |f_{a,1}(\theta) (f_{a,1}(0) - 1) + f_{b,1}(\theta) f_{b,1}(0)|$$

$$\leq |f_{a,1}(\theta)| |f_{a,1}(0) - 1| + \sqrt{1 - f_{a,1}^2(\theta)} \sqrt{1 - f_{a,1}^2(0)}$$

$$\leq 1 \cdot \epsilon + \sqrt{1 - (1 - \epsilon)^2} \sqrt{1 - (1 - \epsilon)^2} = 3\epsilon - \epsilon^2,$$
(358)

which implies

$$|f_{a,2}(\theta) - 1| \le 4\epsilon - \epsilon^2. \tag{359}$$

## PDF page 78


Combining with the obvious requirement that  $|f_{a,2}| \leq 1$ , we arrive at

$$1 - 4\epsilon \le 1 - 4\epsilon + \epsilon^2 \le f_{a,2}(\theta) \le 1. \tag{360}$$

When  $\theta \in [0, \arccos(\gamma)]$ , our ideal operator is I, so the error can be bounded as

$$||I - ((f_{a,2}(\theta)I + if_{b,2}(\theta)Z + if_{c,2}(\theta)X + if_{d,2}(\theta)Y))||$$

$$\leq \sqrt{(1 - f_{a,2}(\theta))^2 + f_{b,2}^2(\theta) + f_{c,2}^2(\theta) + f_{d,2}^2(\theta)}$$

$$\leq \sqrt{(4\epsilon)^2 + 1 - (1 - 4\epsilon)^2} = 2\sqrt{2\epsilon}.$$
(361)

Altogether, we have established the following proposition. Note it is important that the output state  $|\xi_u\rangle$  of gapped phase estimation has no dependence on the  $\pm$  branches of eigenphases. This is the primary goal of branch marking.

**Proposition 23** (Branch marked gapped phase estimation). Let  $A/\alpha_A$  be block encoded by  $O_A$  with normalization factor  $\alpha_A \geq 2 \|A\|$ . Let  $A|\phi_u\rangle = \lambda_u|\phi_u\rangle$  and  $W|\phi_{u,Y\pm}\rangle = e^{\pm i \arccos\left(\frac{\lambda_u}{\alpha_A}\right)}|\phi_{u,Y\pm}\rangle$  be the corresponding eigenpairs of A and the quantum walk operator W. For any  $\epsilon > 0$ ,  $0 < \gamma < \frac{\alpha_A}{2}$  and constant  $\rho > 0$ , the isometry

$$|\pm\rangle|0\rangle|\phi_{u,Y\pm}\rangle \mapsto |\pm\rangle|\xi_{u}\rangle|\phi_{u,Y\pm}\rangle, \qquad \begin{cases} |||\xi_{u}\rangle - |0\rangle|| \leq \epsilon, & \frac{\lambda_{u}}{\alpha_{A}} \in [\gamma, 1), \\ |||\xi_{u}\rangle - i|1\rangle|| \leq \epsilon, & \frac{\lambda_{u}}{\alpha_{A}} \in \left[-\frac{\gamma}{\rho}, \frac{\gamma}{\rho}\right], \\ |||\xi_{u}\rangle - (-|0\rangle)|| \leq \epsilon, & \frac{\lambda_{u}}{\alpha_{A}} \in (-1, -\gamma], \end{cases}$$
(362)

can be implemented using

$$\mathbf{O}\left(\frac{1}{\gamma}\log\left(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\right)\right) \tag{363}$$

queries to  $O_A$ .

*Proof.* By Lemma 20 and the proceeding analysis, we can implement the operators

$$V_{+} = \sum_{u} F(\theta_{u,+}) \otimes |\phi_{u,Y+}\rangle \langle \phi_{u,Y+}|, \qquad V_{-} = \sum_{u} F(-\theta_{u,-}) \otimes |\phi_{u,Y-}\rangle \langle \phi_{u,Y-}|$$
(364)

respectively, such that

$$\begin{cases}
||F(\theta) - I|| \le 2\sqrt{2\epsilon}, & \theta \in (0, \arccos(\gamma)], \\
||F(\theta) - iX|| \le \sqrt{2\epsilon}, & \theta \in \left[\arccos\left(\frac{\gamma}{\rho}\right), \pi - \arccos\left(\frac{\gamma}{\rho}\right)\right], \\
||F(\theta) - (-I)|| \le 2\sqrt{2\epsilon}, & \theta \in [\pi - \arccos(\gamma), \pi).
\end{cases}$$
(365)

Then, the controlled unitary

$$|+\rangle\langle+|\otimes V_{+} + |-\rangle\langle-|\otimes V_{-} \tag{366}$$

implements the mapping

$$|\pm\rangle|0\rangle|\phi_{u,Y\pm}\rangle \mapsto \begin{cases} |\pm\rangle|0\rangle|\phi_{u,Y\pm}\rangle, & \frac{\lambda_u}{\alpha_A} \in [\gamma, 1), \\ i|\pm\rangle|1\rangle|\phi_{u,Y\pm}\rangle, & \frac{\lambda_u}{\alpha_A} \in \left[-\frac{\gamma}{\rho}, \frac{\gamma}{\rho}\right], \\ -|\pm\rangle|0\rangle|\phi_{u,Y\pm}\rangle, & \frac{\lambda_u}{\alpha_A} \in (-1, -\gamma], \end{cases}$$
(367)

to accuracy  $2\sqrt{2\epsilon}$ . The query complexity follows now from Eq. (311) and the rescaling  $2\sqrt{2\epsilon} \mapsto \epsilon$ .

## PDF page 79


# E Multiplicative approximation of success probabilities

In this appendix, we will consider a number of success probabilities involved in the analysis of our quantum linear system algorithm in Section 4. Specifically, we will analyze five different probabilities and show that they are in fact all constant multiplicative approximations of one another.

#### E.1 Definition of success probabilities

The first probability corresponds to the success probability of applying a block-encoded inverse of the input matrix to the initial state. Suppose that our input matrix is block encoded as  $A/\alpha_A$  with normalization factor  $\alpha_A \geq ||A||$  and upper bound  $\alpha_{A^{-1}} \geq ||A^{-1}||$  on norm of the inverse matrix. Without loss of generality, we assume that both  $\alpha_A$  and  $\alpha_{A^{-1}}$  are powers of 3, so that  $\alpha_A\alpha_{A^{-1}} = 3^m$  for some positive integer m. By switching to the Hermitian dilation  $|0\rangle\langle 1| \otimes A + |1\rangle\langle 0| \otimes A^{\dagger}$ , we further assume that A is Hermitian with the spectral decomposition  $A = \sum_u \lambda_u |\phi_u\rangle\langle\phi_u|$  where  $\frac{1}{\alpha_{A^{-1}}} \leq |\lambda_u| \leq \alpha_A$ , while the initial state can be expanded in the eigenbasis of A as  $|b\rangle = \sum_u \gamma_u |\phi_u\rangle$ . Then, we define

$$\psi_{\text{inv}} = \frac{A^{-1}}{\alpha_{A^{-1}}} |b\rangle = \sum_{u} \frac{1}{\alpha_{A^{-1}} \lambda_{u}} \gamma_{u} |\phi_{u}\rangle, \qquad p_{\text{succ}} = \frac{\|A^{-1} |b\rangle\|^{2}}{\alpha_{A^{-1}}^{2}} = \frac{1}{\alpha_{A^{-1}}^{2}} \left(\sum_{u} \left|\frac{\gamma_{u}}{\lambda_{u}}\right|^{2}\right). \tag{368}$$

The second probability is an auxiliary probability for a version of discretized inverse state supported only on one basis state of the time register. We let

$$\psi_{\text{d-inv},1} = \sum_{k=0}^{m-1} \frac{3^{k+1}}{3^m} |k\rangle \sum_{\left|\frac{\lambda_u}{\alpha_A}\right| \in \left[\frac{1}{3^{k+1}}, \frac{1}{3^k}\right)} \gamma_u |\phi_u\rangle, \qquad p_{\text{succ,d-inv},1} = \sum_{k=0}^{m-1} \sum_{\left|\frac{\lambda_u}{\alpha_A}\right| \in \left[\frac{1}{3^{k+1}}, \frac{1}{3^k}\right)} |\gamma_u|^2 \frac{9^{k+1}}{9^m}.$$

$$(369)$$

The third probability corresponds to the probability of the discretized inverse state supported on two basis states of the time register. Concretely,

$$\psi_{\text{d-inv}} = \sum_{k=0}^{m-1} \sum_{\left|\frac{\lambda_{u}}{\alpha_{A}}\right| \in \left[\frac{1}{3^{k+1}}, \frac{1}{3^{k}}\right)} \left(\zeta_{k+1, u} \frac{3^{k+1}}{3^{m}} |k\rangle + \zeta_{k, u} \frac{3^{k}}{3^{m}} |k-1\rangle\right) \gamma_{u} |\phi_{u}\rangle,$$

$$p_{\text{succ,d-inv}} = \sum_{k=0}^{m-1} \sum_{\left|\frac{\lambda_{u}}{\alpha_{A}}\right| \in \left[\frac{1}{3^{k+1}}, \frac{1}{3^{k}}\right)} |\gamma_{u}|^{2} \sum_{j=k, k+1} |\zeta_{j, u}|^{2} \frac{9^{j}}{9^{m}}.$$

$$(370)$$

The fourth probability is also an auxiliary probability for a version of discretized inverse state with full support on all basis states of the time register, assuming branch marking is performed perfectly:

$$\psi_{\text{d-inv},m} = \sum_{k=0}^{m-1} \sum_{\left|\frac{\lambda_{u}}{\alpha_{A}}\right| \in \left[\frac{1}{3^{k+1}}, \frac{1}{3^{k}}\right)} \sum_{j=1}^{m} \zeta_{j,u} \frac{3^{j}}{3^{m}} |j-1\rangle \gamma_{u} |\phi_{u}\rangle,$$

$$p_{\text{succ,d-inv},m} = \sum_{k=0}^{m-1} \sum_{\left|\frac{\lambda_{u}}{\alpha_{A}}\right| \in \left[\frac{1}{3^{k+1}}, \frac{1}{3^{k}}\right)} |\gamma_{u}|^{2} \sum_{j=1}^{m} |\zeta_{j,u}|^{2} \frac{9^{j}}{9^{m}}.$$
(371)

Finally, the fifth probability is for the actual state  $\psi_{\text{d-inv,bm}}$  prepared by the erroneous GPE and branch marking. We denote this probability by  $p_{\text{succ.d-inv,bm}}$ .

## PDF page 80


#### E.2 Multiplicative bounds on success probabilities

We now give multiplicative bounds on the success probabilities introduced in the previous subsection. Our analysis uses the following lemma.

**Lemma 24.** The following estimates hold:

$$\sum_{k=0}^{m-1} \sum_{\left|\frac{\lambda_{u}}{\alpha_{A}}\right| \in \left[\frac{1}{3^{k+1}}, \frac{1}{3^{k}}\right)} |\gamma_{u}|^{2} \sum_{j \neq k, k+1} |\zeta_{j,u}|^{2} \frac{9^{j}}{9^{m}} \leq \left(\sum_{k=0}^{m-1} \sum_{\left|\frac{\lambda_{u}}{\alpha_{A}}\right| \in \left[\frac{1}{3^{k+1}}, \frac{1}{3^{k}}\right)} |\gamma_{u}|^{2} \frac{9^{k+2}}{9^{m}}\right) \epsilon_{gpe}^{2},$$

$$\sum_{k=0}^{m-1} \sum_{\left|\frac{\lambda_{u}}{\alpha_{A}}\right| \in \left[\frac{1}{3^{k+1}}, \frac{1}{3^{k}}\right)} |\gamma_{u}|^{2} \sum_{j \neq k, k+1} |\zeta_{j,u}|^{2} \frac{9^{k}}{9^{m}} \leq \left(\sum_{k=0}^{m-1} \sum_{\left|\frac{\lambda_{u}}{\alpha_{A}}\right| \in \left[\frac{1}{3^{k+1}}, \frac{1}{3^{k}}\right)} |\gamma_{u}|^{2} \frac{9^{k}}{9^{m}}\right) \epsilon_{gpe}^{2}.$$
(372)

*Proof.* Recall that the cumulative coefficients  $\zeta_{j,u}$  have the following bound

$$|\zeta_{j,u}| \le \begin{cases} \epsilon_{\text{gpe},j}, & j \le k-1, \\ \prod_{l=k+1}^{j-1} \epsilon_{\text{gpe},l}, & j \ge k+2. \end{cases}$$

$$(373)$$

Therefore, we can prove the first claim as

$$\sum_{k=0}^{m-1} \sum_{\left|\frac{\lambda_{u}}{\alpha_{A}}\right| \in \left[\frac{1}{3^{k+1}}, \frac{1}{3^{k}}\right)} |\gamma_{u}|^{2} \left(\sum_{j=1}^{k-1} \epsilon_{\text{gpe},j}^{2} \frac{9^{j}}{9^{m}} + \sum_{j=k+2}^{m} \prod_{l=k+1}^{j-1} \epsilon_{\text{gpe},l}^{2} \frac{9^{j}}{9^{m}}\right) \\
\leq \sum_{k=0}^{m-1} \sum_{\left|\frac{\lambda_{u}}{\alpha_{A}}\right| \in \left[\frac{1}{3^{k+1}}, \frac{1}{3^{k}}\right)} |\gamma_{u}|^{2} \left(\sum_{j=1}^{k-1} \epsilon_{\text{gpe},j}^{2} \frac{9^{k-1}}{9^{m}} + \sum_{j=k+2}^{m} \epsilon_{\text{gpe},j-1}^{2} \frac{9^{k+2}}{9^{m}}\right) \\
\leq \sum_{k=0}^{m-1} \sum_{\left|\frac{\lambda_{u}}{\alpha_{A}}\right| \in \left[\frac{1}{3^{k+1}}, \frac{1}{3^{k}}\right)} |\gamma_{u}|^{2} \sum_{j=1}^{m-1} \epsilon_{\text{gpe},j}^{2} \frac{9^{k+2}}{9^{m}} \leq \sum_{k=0}^{m-1} \sum_{\left|\frac{\lambda_{u}}{\alpha_{A}}\right| \in \left[\frac{1}{3^{k+1}}, \frac{1}{3^{k}}\right)} |\gamma_{u}|^{2} \frac{9^{k+2}}{9^{m}} \underbrace{\left(\sum_{j=1}^{m-1} \epsilon_{\text{gpe},j}^{2}\right)^{2}}_{\epsilon_{2m}^{2}},$$

$$(374)$$

where we have assumed all  $\epsilon_{\mathrm{gpe},l} \leq \frac{1}{3}$ . Similarly for the second claim,

$$\sum_{k=0}^{m-1} \sum_{\left|\frac{\lambda_{u}}{\alpha_{A}}\right| \in \left[\frac{1}{3^{k+1}}, \frac{1}{3^{k}}\right)} |\gamma_{u}|^{2} \left(\sum_{j=1}^{k-1} \epsilon_{\text{gpe},j}^{2} \frac{9^{k}}{9^{m}} + \sum_{j=k+2}^{m} \prod_{l=k+1}^{j-1} \epsilon_{\text{gpe},l}^{2} \frac{9^{k}}{9^{m}}\right) \\
\leq \sum_{k=0}^{m-1} \sum_{\left|\frac{\lambda_{u}}{\alpha_{A}}\right| \in \left[\frac{1}{3^{k+1}}, \frac{1}{3^{k}}\right)} |\gamma_{u}|^{2} \left(\sum_{j=1}^{k-1} \epsilon_{\text{gpe},j}^{2} \frac{9^{k}}{9^{m}} + \sum_{j=k+2}^{m} \epsilon_{\text{gpe},j-1}^{2} \frac{9^{k}}{9^{m}}\right) \\
\leq \sum_{k=0}^{m-1} \sum_{\left|\frac{\lambda_{u}}{\alpha_{A}}\right| \in \left[\frac{1}{3^{k+1}}, \frac{1}{3^{k}}\right)} |\gamma_{u}|^{2} \sum_{j=1}^{m-1} \epsilon_{\text{gpe},j}^{2} \frac{9^{k}}{9^{m}} \leq \sum_{k=0}^{m-1} \sum_{\left|\frac{\lambda_{u}}{\alpha_{A}}\right| \in \left[\frac{1}{3^{k+1}}, \frac{1}{3^{k}}\right)} |\gamma_{u}|^{2} \frac{9^{k}}{9^{m}} \left(\sum_{j=1}^{m-1} \epsilon_{\text{gpe},j}\right)^{2}. \tag{375}$$

## PDF page 81


Let us begin with the relation between  $p_{\text{succ}}$  and  $p_{\text{succ},d-\text{inv},1}$  which is easy to describe. Following

$$\frac{1}{\alpha_{A-1}^{2}} \sum_{k=0}^{m-1} \sum_{\left|\frac{\lambda_{u}}{\alpha_{A}}\right| \in \left[\frac{1}{3^{k+1}}, \frac{1}{3^{k}}\right)} \left|\frac{\gamma_{u}}{\lambda_{u}}\right|^{2} \leq \frac{1}{\alpha_{A-1}^{2}} \sum_{k=0}^{m-1} \sum_{\left|\frac{\lambda_{u}}{\alpha_{A}}\right| \in \left[\frac{1}{3^{k+1}}, \frac{1}{3^{k}}\right)} |\gamma_{u}|^{2} \frac{9^{k+1}}{\alpha_{A}^{2}} = \sum_{k=0}^{m-1} \sum_{\left|\frac{\lambda_{u}}{\alpha_{A}}\right| \in \left[\frac{1}{3^{k+1}}, \frac{1}{3^{k}}\right)} |\gamma_{u}|^{2} \frac{9^{k+1}}{9^{m}},$$

$$\frac{1}{\alpha_{A-1}^{2}} \sum_{k=0}^{m-1} \sum_{\left|\frac{\lambda_{u}}{\alpha_{A}}\right| \in \left[\frac{1}{3^{k+1}}, \frac{1}{3^{k}}\right)} \left|\frac{\gamma_{u}}{\lambda_{u}}\right|^{2} \geq \frac{1}{\alpha_{A-1}^{2}} \sum_{k=0}^{m-1} \sum_{\left|\frac{\lambda_{u}}{\alpha_{A}}\right| \in \left[\frac{1}{3^{k+1}}, \frac{1}{3^{k}}\right)} |\gamma_{u}|^{2} \frac{9^{k}}{9^{m}},$$

$$(376)$$

we conclude that

$$\frac{p_{\text{succ,d-inv,1}}}{9} \le p_{\text{succ}} \le p_{\text{succ,d-inv,1}}.$$
(377)

Then, we consider  $p_{\text{succ,d-inv},1}$  and  $p_{\text{succ,d-inv}}$ . It is fairly straightforward to derive one side of the bound

$$\sum_{k=0}^{m-1} \sum_{\left|\frac{\lambda_{u}}{\alpha_{A}}\right| \in \left[\frac{1}{3^{k+1}}, \frac{1}{3^{k}}\right)} |\gamma_{u}|^{2} \sum_{j=k, k+1} |\zeta_{j, u}|^{2} \frac{9^{j}}{9^{m}} \leq \sum_{k=0}^{m-1} \sum_{\left|\frac{\lambda_{u}}{\alpha_{A}}\right| \in \left[\frac{1}{3^{k+1}}, \frac{1}{3^{k}}\right)} |\gamma_{u}|^{2} \frac{9^{k+1}}{9^{m}}.$$
 (378)

Analysis of the other side however is more involved and relies on Lemma 24:

$$\sum_{k=0}^{m-1} \sum_{\left|\frac{\lambda_{u}}{\alpha_{A}}\right| \in \left[\frac{1}{3^{k+1}}, \frac{1}{3^{k}}\right)} |\gamma_{u}|^{2} \sum_{j=k,k+1} |\zeta_{j,u}|^{2} \frac{9^{j}}{9^{m}} \ge \sum_{k=0}^{m-1} \sum_{\left|\frac{\lambda_{u}}{\alpha_{A}}\right| \in \left[\frac{1}{3^{k+1}}, \frac{1}{3^{k}}\right)} |\gamma_{u}|^{2} \sum_{j=k,k+1} |\zeta_{j,u}|^{2} \frac{9^{k}}{9^{m}} \\
= \sum_{k=0}^{m-1} \sum_{\left|\frac{\lambda_{u}}{\alpha_{A}}\right| \in \left[\frac{1}{3^{k+1}}, \frac{1}{3^{k}}\right)} |\gamma_{u}|^{2} \sum_{j=1}^{m} |\zeta_{j,u}|^{2} \frac{9^{k}}{9^{m}} - \sum_{k=0}^{m-1} \sum_{\left|\frac{\lambda_{u}}{\alpha_{A}}\right| \in \left[\frac{1}{3^{k+1}}, \frac{1}{3^{k}}\right)} |\gamma_{u}|^{2} \sum_{j\neq k,k+1} |\zeta_{j,u}|^{2} \frac{9^{k}}{9^{m}} \\
\ge \frac{p_{\text{succ,d-inv},1}}{9} - \frac{p_{\text{succ,d-inv},1}}{9} \epsilon_{\text{gpe}}^{2}.$$
(379)

This gives

$$\frac{p_{\text{succ,d-inv},1}}{9} \left( 1 - \epsilon_{\text{gpe}}^2 \right) \le p_{\text{succ,d-inv}} \le p_{\text{succ,d-inv},1}. \tag{380}$$

Next, we discuss  $p_{\text{succ,d-inv}}$  and  $p_{\text{succ,d-inv},m}$ . Again, one side of the bound follows trivially from the definition

$$\sum_{k=0}^{m-1} \sum_{\left|\frac{\lambda_{u}}{\alpha_{A}}\right| \in \left[\frac{1}{3^{k+1}}, \frac{1}{3^{k}}\right)} |\gamma_{u}|^{2} \sum_{j=k, k+1} |\zeta_{j, u}|^{2} \frac{9^{j}}{9^{m}} \leq \sum_{k=0}^{m-1} \sum_{\left|\frac{\lambda_{u}}{\alpha_{A}}\right| \in \left[\frac{1}{3^{k+1}}, \frac{1}{3^{k}}\right)} |\gamma_{u}|^{2} \sum_{j=1}^{m} |\zeta_{j, u}|^{2} \frac{9^{j}}{9^{m}}.$$
(381)

As for the other side, we apply Lemma 24 to compute

$$\sum_{k=0}^{m-1} \sum_{\left|\frac{\lambda_{u}}{\alpha_{A}}\right| \in \left[\frac{1}{3^{k+1}}, \frac{1}{3^{k}}\right)} |\gamma_{u}|^{2} \sum_{j=k,k+1} |\zeta_{j,u}|^{2} \frac{9^{j}}{9^{m}}$$

$$= \sum_{k=0}^{m-1} \sum_{\left|\frac{\lambda_{u}}{\alpha_{A}}\right| \in \left[\frac{1}{3^{k+1}}, \frac{1}{3^{k}}\right)} |\gamma_{u}|^{2} \sum_{j=1}^{m} |\zeta_{j,u}|^{2} \frac{9^{j}}{9^{m}} - \sum_{k=0}^{m-1} \sum_{\left|\frac{\lambda_{u}}{\alpha_{A}}\right| \in \left[\frac{1}{3^{k+1}}, \frac{1}{3^{k}}\right)} |\gamma_{u}|^{2} \sum_{j\neq k,k+1} |\zeta_{j,u}|^{2} \frac{9^{j}}{9^{m}}$$

$$\geq p_{\text{succ,d-inv},m} - 9^{2} p_{\text{succ,d-inv},1} \epsilon_{\text{gpe}}^{2} \geq p_{\text{succ,d-inv},m} - 9^{3} p_{\text{succ,d-inv}} \left(\frac{\epsilon_{\text{gpe}}}{1 - \epsilon_{\text{gpe}}}\right)^{2}.$$
(382)

## PDF page 82


We thus obtain

$$p_{\text{succ,d-inv}} \le p_{\text{succ,d-inv},m} \le p_{\text{succ,d-inv}} \left( 1 + 9^3 \frac{\epsilon_{\text{gpe}}^2}{(1 - \epsilon_{\text{gpe}})^2} \right).$$
 (383)

Finally, we examine  $p_{\text{succ,d-inv,}m}$  and  $p_{\text{succ,d-inv,bm}}$ . As branch marking is invoked twice, we have the additive approximation

$$|p_{\text{succ,d-inv,}m} - p_{\text{succ,d-inv,bm}}| \le 2 \left| \sqrt{p_{\text{succ,d-inv,}m}} - \sqrt{p_{\text{succ,d-inv,bm}}} \right| \le 4\epsilon_{\text{bm}}.$$
 (384)

Let us summarize these estimates as follows.

**Proposition 25** (Multiplicative bounds on success probabilities). Let the five success probabilities  $p_{succ, d-inv, 1}$ ,  $p_{succ, d-inv}$ ,  $p_{succ, d-inv, m}$  and  $p_{succ, d-inv, bm}$  be defined as in Appendix E.1. Then,

$$\frac{p_{succ,d-inv,1}}{9} \leq p_{succ} \leq p_{succ,d-inv,1},$$

$$\frac{p_{succ,d-inv,1}}{9} \left(1 - \epsilon_{gpe}^{2}\right) \leq p_{succ,d-inv} \leq p_{succ,d-inv,1},$$

$$p_{succ,d-inv} \leq p_{succ,d-inv,m} \leq p_{succ,d-inv} \left(1 + 9^{3} \frac{\epsilon_{gpe}^{2}}{\left(1 - \epsilon_{gpe}\right)^{2}}\right),$$

$$|p_{succ,d-inv,m} - p_{succ,d-inv,bm}| \leq 4\epsilon_{bm}.$$
(385)

Hence, as long as

$$\epsilon_{qpe} = \mathbf{O}(1), \qquad \epsilon_{bm} = \mathbf{O}\left(p_{succ}\right), \tag{386}$$

all success probabilities are constant multiplicative approximations of one another

$$p_{succ,d-inv,1}, p_{succ,d-inv}, p_{succ,d-inv,m}, p_{succ,d-inv,bm} = \Theta(p_{succ}).$$
 (387)

#### E.3 Multiplicative bounds on sum of amplification thresholds

In Section 4.2, we have derived the following multiplicative bounds on sum of amplification thresholds

$$p_{\text{succ,d-inv}} 9^l \le \sum_{j=m-l+1}^m \left\| \overline{\Pi_j \Pi_b} A_j \cdots A_1 |\psi_0\rangle \right\|^2 9^{j-m+l} \le \frac{5}{4} p_{\text{succ,d-inv}} 9^l, \tag{388}$$

where we assume VTAA with input algorithms  $A_1, \ldots, A_m$  produces  $\psi_{\text{d-inv}}$ . We now incorporate the error of GPE and branch marking into the analysis.

When we consider erroneous GPE and perfect branch marking, VTAA with input algorithms  $B_1, \ldots, B_m$  produces  $\psi_{\text{d-inv},m}$  and the potentially good probabilities take the form

$$\left\| \overline{\Pi_{j}} \overline{\Pi_{b}} B_{j} \cdots B_{1} |\psi_{0}\rangle \right\|^{2} = \sum_{k=0}^{m-1} \sum_{\left|\frac{\lambda_{u}}{\alpha_{A}}\right| \in \left[\frac{1}{3^{k+1}}, \frac{1}{3^{k}}\right)} |\gamma_{u}|^{2} \sum_{h=j+1}^{m} |\zeta_{h,u}|^{2} + \sum_{k=0}^{m-1} \sum_{\left|\frac{\lambda_{u}}{\alpha_{A}}\right| \in \left[\frac{1}{3^{k+1}}, \frac{1}{3^{k}}\right)} |\gamma_{u}|^{2} \sum_{h=1}^{j} |\zeta_{h,u}|^{2} \frac{9^{h}}{9^{m}}.$$
(389)

## PDF page 83


Correspondingly, the sum of amplification thresholds are split into two terms:

$$\sum_{j=m-l+1}^{m} \left\| \overline{\Pi_{j}} \overline{\Pi_{b}} B_{j} \cdots B_{1} |\psi_{0}\rangle \right\|^{2} 9^{j-m+l} = \sum_{j=m-l+1}^{m} \sum_{k=0}^{m-1} \sum_{\left|\frac{\lambda_{u}}{\alpha_{A}}\right| \in \left[\frac{1}{3^{k+1}}, \frac{1}{3^{k}}\right)} |\gamma_{u}|^{2} \sum_{h=j+1}^{m} |\zeta_{h,u}|^{2} 9^{j-m+l} + \sum_{j=m-l+1}^{m} \sum_{k=0}^{m-1} \sum_{\left|\frac{\lambda_{u}}{\alpha_{A}}\right| \in \left[\frac{1}{3^{k+1}}, \frac{1}{3^{k}}\right)} |\gamma_{u}|^{2} \sum_{h=1}^{j} |\zeta_{h,u}|^{2} \frac{9^{h}}{9^{m}} 9^{j-m+l}.$$
(390)

We exchange the summation order in the first term to get

$$\sum_{j=m-l+1}^{m} \sum_{h=j+1}^{m} 9^{j} = \sum_{h=m-l+2}^{m} \sum_{j=m-l+1}^{h-1} 9^{j} = \frac{9}{8} \sum_{h=m-l+2}^{m} 9^{h-1},$$
(391)

whereas for the second term

$$\sum_{j=m-l+1}^{m} \sum_{h=1}^{j} 9^{j} = \sum_{h=1}^{m} \sum_{j=h}^{m} 9^{j} = \frac{9}{8} \sum_{h=1}^{m} 9^{m}.$$
 (392)

Altogether,

$$p_{\text{succ,d-inv},m} 9^{l} \leq \sum_{j=m-l+1}^{m} \left\| \overline{\Pi_{j}} \overline{\Pi_{b}} B_{j} \cdots B_{1} |\psi_{0}\rangle \right\|^{2} 9^{j-m+l}$$

$$\leq \frac{5}{4} \sum_{k=0}^{m-1} \sum_{\left|\frac{\lambda_{u}}{\alpha_{A}}\right| \in \left[\frac{1}{3^{k+1}}, \frac{1}{3^{k}}\right)} |\gamma_{u}|^{2} \sum_{h=1}^{m} |\zeta_{h,u}|^{2} 9^{h-m+l} \leq \frac{5}{4} p_{\text{succ,d-inv},m} 9^{l}.$$
(393)

Finally, we consider the general case where both GPE and branch marking are imperfect. In this case, VTAA with input algorithms  $C_1, \ldots, C_m$  produces  $\psi_{\text{d-inv,bm}}$  with  $\|\psi_{\text{d-inv,bm}}\|^2 = p_{\text{succ,d-inv,bm}}$ . Then, the sum of amplification thresholds has error

$$\left| \sum_{j=m-l+1}^{m} \left\| \overline{\Pi_{j}} \overline{\Pi_{b}} B_{j} \cdots B_{1} |\psi_{0}\rangle \right\|^{2} 9^{j-m+l} - \sum_{j=m-l+1}^{m} \left\| \overline{\Pi_{j}} \overline{\Pi_{b}} C_{j} \cdots C_{1} |\psi_{0}\rangle \right\|^{2} 9^{j-m+l} \right| \leq \frac{9}{8} 4 \epsilon_{\rm bm} 9^{l}. \quad (394)$$

We thus obtain:

**Proposition 26** (Multiplicative bounds on sum of amplification thresholds). Let the input algorithms  $A_1, \ldots, A_m, B_1, \ldots, B_m$  and  $C_1, \ldots, C_m$  be defined as in Appendix E.3. Then,

$$p_{succ,d-inv}9^{l} \leq \sum_{j=m-l+1}^{m} \left\| \overline{\Pi_{j}} \overline{\Pi_{b}} A_{j} \cdots A_{1} |\psi_{0}\rangle \right\|^{2} 9^{j-m+l} \leq \frac{5}{4} p_{succ,d-inv}9^{l},$$

$$p_{succ,d-inv,m}9^{l} \leq \sum_{j=m-l+1}^{m} \left\| \overline{\Pi_{j}} \overline{\Pi_{b}} B_{j} \cdots B_{1} |\psi_{0}\rangle \right\|^{2} 9^{j-m+l} \leq \frac{5}{4} p_{succ,d-inv,m}9^{l},$$

$$\left| \sum_{j=m-l+1}^{m} \left\| \overline{\Pi_{j}} \overline{\Pi_{b}} B_{j} \cdots B_{1} |\psi_{0}\rangle \right\|^{2} 9^{j-m+l} - \sum_{j=m-l+1}^{m} \left\| \overline{\Pi_{j}} \overline{\Pi_{b}} C_{j} \cdots C_{1} |\psi_{0}\rangle \right\|^{2} 9^{j-m+l} \right| \leq \frac{9}{8} 4 \epsilon_{bm} 9^{l}.$$

$$(395)$$

## PDF page 84


Hence, as long as

$$\epsilon_{qpe} = \mathbf{O}(1), \qquad \epsilon_{bm} = \mathbf{O}\left(p_{succ}\right),$$
(396)

all sums of amplification thresholds have the scaling

$$\sum_{j=m-l+1}^{m} \left\| \overline{\Pi_{j}} \overline{\Pi_{b}} A_{j} \cdots A_{1} |\psi_{0}\rangle \right\|^{2} 9^{j-m+l} = \mathbf{\Theta} \left( p_{succ} 9^{l} \right),$$

$$\sum_{j=m-l+1}^{m} \left\| \overline{\Pi_{j}} \overline{\Pi_{b}} B_{j} \cdots B_{1} |\psi_{0}\rangle \right\|^{2} 9^{j-m+l} = \mathbf{\Theta} \left( p_{succ} 9^{l} \right),$$

$$\sum_{j=m-l+1}^{m} \left\| \overline{\Pi_{j}} \overline{\Pi_{b}} C_{j} \cdots C_{1} |\psi_{0}\rangle \right\|^{2} 9^{j-m+l} = \mathbf{\Theta} \left( p_{succ} 9^{l} \right).$$
(397)
